2. External Oil System Inspection
(Figure 601), (Figure 602), (Figure 603), (Figure 604)
A. General
(1) This task is a visual check of the engine and nacelle for evidence of oil leakage.
B. References
Reference Title
71-00-02-000-801-F00 Power Plant - Removal (P/B 401)
71-00-02-400-801-F00 Power Plant - Installation (P/B 401)
71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
71-71-00-200-801-F00 Engine Vents and Drains Inspection (P/B 601)
78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
411 Engine 1 - Engine
421 Engine 2 - Engine
D. Do a Check for the External Oil Leakage
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-001-F00
(1) With the engine stopped, do a check of the bottom of the fan cowls for oil leakage at these
locations (Figure 601):
(a) The starter air discharge duct.
(b) The forward drain holes.
(c) The aft drain holes.
79-00-00
SUBTASK 79-00-00-010-001-F00
(2) If you find oil leakage, do this task: Engine Vents and Drains Inspection,
TASK 71-71-00-200-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-005-F00
(3) Do a check of the inner side of the primary nozzle on the turbine frame struts and the stage 4
LPT blades for oil leakage (Figure 602).
(a) If they are wet with oil and the oil consumption is above serviceable limits, replace the
engine.
These are the tasks:
• Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00
• Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-006-F00
(4) Do a check of the fan blades and the stage 1 vanes at the 6:00 o'clock position for oil leakage
(Figure 603).
(a) If they are wet with oil, replace the engine.
These are the tasks:
• Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00
• Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-010-002-F00
(5) If the fan cowl panels are not open, do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels,
TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-007-F00
(6) Do a check of these components to see if they are wet with oil:
(a) The fan cowl panels.
(b) The engine surfaces.
(c) The oil system components.
(d) The fan frame interface points with the transfer gearbox.
(e) If they are wet with oil, replace the applicable engine component.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-010-008-F00
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU
OPEN THE THRUST REVERSER: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE
DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST
REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL
PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(7) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-008-F00
(8) Do a check of the oil scavenge and the oil supply tubes for damage or for loose connections
(Figure 604):
NOTE: Look for oil stripes and drops of oil.
(a) Tubes and tube coupling nuts with cracks are not serviceable.
79-00-00
(b) There is no limit to the number of nicks, scratches, chafing or scores that are permitted if
the depth is less than 0.01 inch (0.3 mm) (after you remove the high metal).
(c) Two dents for each tube is permitted if they have a smooth contour and the depth is less
than 0.05 inch (1.30 mm).
(d) Replace or tighten the nuts and bolts which are loose or missing.
(e) Replace or repair the tube clamps which are loose or worn.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-410-009-F00
WARNING
OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE THE THRUST
REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS, INJURIES TO
PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(9) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.
79-00-00-200-803-F00Internal Oil System InspectionPDF p. 20
Open original AMM PDF - page 20 ↗ 
3. Internal Oil System Inspection
A. General
(1) This task is a borescope inspection of areas of the internal engine where oil leakage could
occur. When you do the fault isolation for an oil consumption problem, an inspection for an
internal oil leak could be necessary.
(2) This task includes a borescope inspection of the High Pressure Compressor (HPC) and an
inspection of the scavenge screens and chip detectors.
B. References
Reference Title
71-00-00-800-806-F00 Engine Operation Limits (P/B 201)
71-00-02-000-801-F00 Power Plant - Removal (P/B 401)
71-00-02-400-801-F00 Power Plant - Installation (P/B 401)
72-00-00-200-804-F00 HP Compressor Blades Borescope Inspection (P/B 601)
78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

E. Aft Sump Oil System Inspection
SUBTASK 79-00-00-360-001-F01
(1) If there is oil leakage or large amount of coking at the Turbine Rear Frame (TRF) interface, do
these steps:
(a) Do this task: Primary Nozzle Assembly Removal, TASK 78-11-01-000-802-F00 or Primary
Nozzle Assembly Removal, TASK 78-11-01-000-801-F00.
(b) Do this task: Primary Plug Assembly Removal, TASK 78-11-02-000-802-F00 or Primary
Plug Assembly Removal, TASK 78-11-02-000-801-F00.
(c) Remove the flame arrestor, refer to: TASK 72-56-00-000-802-F00.
(d) Remove the flange assembly, refer to: TASK 72-56-00-000-802-F00.
(e) Examine the oil supply coupling nuts for loose connections.
RYR ALL PRE SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0676
NOTE: Do not touch, disconnect, or tighten the scavenge tube coupling nut to the TRF
scavenge nipple unless you have the counter wrench, SPL-4968. Damage to the
equipment can occur which can lead to oil loss.
RYR ALL POST SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0676
NOTE: Do not touch, disconnect, or tighten the scavenge tube coupling nut to the TRF
scavenge nipple unless you have the counter wrench, SPL-21063. Damage to
the equipment can occur which can lead to oil loss.
RYR ALL
1) If necessary, tighten the loose coupling nuts.
(f) Examine the oil scavenge tube and connection for cracks.
1) If there is a crack on the scavenge tube or connection, replace the engine.
a) To replace the engine, do these tasks:
• Power Plant - Removal, TASK 71-00-02-000-801-F00.
• Power Plant - Installation, TASK 71-00-02-400-801-F00.
(g) Examine the oil supply tube assembly for cracks.
1) If there is a crack on the oil supply tube that goes through the No. 10 TRF strut, do
this step:
RYR ALL PRE SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0339
a) Do the CFM-SB 72-0339 to install a replaceable oil tube.
RYR ALL POST SB 737-CFM56-7B-72-0339
b) Replace the oil supply tube, do this task: TASK 72-56-00-300-801-F00.
RYR ALL
(h) If there are no cracks on the oil supply tube or loose coupling nuts, replace the oil inlet
cover o-ring and gasket.
1) To replace the o-ring and gasket, do these tasks:
79-00-00
• Turbine Rear Frame (TRF) Oil Inlet Cover Removal,
TASK 72-56-00-000-802-F00.
• Turbine Rear Frame (TRF) Oil Inlet Cover Installation,
TASK 72-56-00-400-801-F00.
(i) Install the flange assembly, refer to: TASK 72-56-00-400-801-F00.
(j) Install the flame arrestor, refer to: TASK 72-56-00-400-801-F00.
(k) Do this task: Primary Plug Assembly Installation, TASK 78-11-02-400-802-F00 or Primary
Plug Assembly Installation, TASK 78-11-02-400-801-F00.
(l) Do this task: Primary Nozzle Assembly Installation, TASK 78-11-01-400-802-F00 or
Primary Nozzle Assembly Installation, TASK 78-11-01-400-801-F00.

5. CDU Interrogation of the DMS Detectors
A. General
(1) This task is a FMCS CDU interrogation of the Debris Monitoring System (DMS) for debris on
the detectors.
B. References
Reference Title
73-21-00-740-803-F00 EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS (P/B 501)
C. CDU Interrogation of the DMS Detectors
SUBTASK 79-00-00-220-003-F00
(1) Do this task: EEC BITE TEST - RECENT FAULTS, TASK 73-21-00-740-803-F00.
(a) Look for one or more of these maintenance messages 79-11151, 79-11152, 79-21151,
79-21152, 79-31151 or 79-31152.
NOTE: This step will make sure that the DMS is serviceable.
1) If one or more of the maintenance messages show, then you must manually
examine the DMS detectors, do this task: Chip Detectors and Scavenge Screens -
Inspection, TASK 79-00-00-200-804-F00
2) If a maintenance message does not show, then continue with subsequent step.
(b) To find out if the DMS has found debris on the detectors, do this task, FIM 79-21 TASK
808, Initial Evaluation only.

6. DMS Operational Check
A. General

Reference Title

79-00-00
RYR ALL; ENGINES WITH DEBRIS MONITORING SYSTEM (Continued)
E. DMS Operational Check
SUBTASK 79-00-00-740-001-F00
(1) Do a check of the DMS STATUS on the Flight Management Computer/Control Display Unit
(FMCS CDU):
(a) Push the INIT REF key two times:
NOTE: This causes the PERF INIT INDEX to show
(b) Push the INDEX line select key (LSK).
(c) Push the MAINT (LSK).
(d) Push the ENGINE LSK.
(e) Push the line select key for the applicable engine (ENGINE 1 or ENGINE 2).
NOTE: This causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST MAIN MENU to show.
(f) Push the DMS STATUS LSK.
NOTE: This causes the ENGINE X BITE TEST DMS STATUS to show.
(g) If DEBRIS DETECTED: YES shows, this indicates debris detection is active, then
continue (FIM 79-21 TASK 808).
(h) If DEBRIS DETECTED: NO shows, this indicates debris detection is not active, then
continue.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-010-009-F00
(2) Open the applicable doors:
Number Name/Location
413BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 1
423BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 2
SUBTASK 79-00-00-710-001-F00
(3) Do these steps for a test of the DMS line:
(a) Remove one DMS detector from the lubrication unit (TASK 79-21-05-000-806-F00 or
TASK 79-21-05-000-807-F00).
(b) Put a clean metallic wire jumper or any metallic piece onto the DMS detector magnet to
bridge the gap.
(c) Do a check of the DMS STATUS on the FMC-CDU. If DEBRIS DETECTED: YES shows,
the line has passed the test.
1) Remove the wire jumper and clean the Detector magnet.
2) Install and lock the DMS detector back into the lubrication unit
(TASK 79-21-05-400-805-F00).
(d) If DEBRIS DETECTED: NO shows, the DMS has failed the test.
NOTE: This is caused by an open circuit within the DMS Detector, the DMS Box or the
J8 Harness.
1) Remove the wire jumper and clean the detector magnet.
2) Do this task: DMS Requires Inspection - Fault Isolation, FIM 79-21 TASK 808, to
determine the cause and correct it.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-710-002-F00
(4) Do the DMS line test above again on the other two DMS detectors, one at a time.
79-00-00
RYR ALL; ENGINES WITH DEBRIS MONITORING SYSTEM (Continued)
SUBTASK 79-00-00-410-010-F00
(5) Close the applicable doors:
Number Name/Location
413BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 1
423BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 2
RYR ALL

7. Chip Detectors and Scavenge Screens - Inspection
(Figure 606, Figure 607, and Figure 608)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) This task provides the instructions on how to do the inspection of the chip detectors and
scavenge screens.
(2) For identification of a bearing failure and other failures of the oil system components, examine
the chip detectors and scavenge screens.
(3) Accumulation, of a specific volume, of particles over a series of regularly scheduled chip
detector inspections is not used as a method to identify any specific engine failure. However,
based on initial chip detector findings, reduced interval chip detector inspections may be
necessary to determine the amount, type, and source of collected particles. Particle
identification is based on visual or laboratory analysis. Corrective action is taken after you have
identified the collected particles.
B. References
Reference Title

F . Chip Detectors and Scavenge Screens Inspection
SUBTASK 79-00-00-020-001-F00
CAUTION
REMOVE AND TAG ONE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR AT A TIME. THIS CAN
PREVENT AN ERROR IN IDENTIFICATION OF THE SOURCE OF THE
MATERIAL ON THE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTORS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
DAMAGE CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector (MCD) Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-806-F00 or Debris
Monitoring System (DMS) Detector Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-807-F00.
RYR ALL; ENGINES WITH DEBRIS MONITORING SYSTEM
NOTE: If the Debris Monitoring System (DMS) detector is removed for inspection only, it is not
necessary to disconnect the DMS detector connector from the DMS box.
RYR ALL
(a) Make sure that there is the DMS round-head plug (small round and flat piece on the plug
handle).
1) If there is a hole, replace DMS as quickly as possible.
(b) Place without dripping, the chip detector or scavenge screen with particles in a new
polyethylene bag.
(c) Attach a tag to identify each chip detector location.
1) The tag identifies the plug location for each sump scavenge circuit (Accessory Gear
Box (AGB)/Transfer Gearbox (TGB) Scavenge, Front FWD Sump Scavenge, AFT
Sump Scavenge).
SUBTASK 79-00-00-020-002-F00
CAUTION
REMOVE THE SCREEN FROM THE CHIP DETECTOR TO EXAMINE THE
CHIP DETECTOR MAGNET FOR THE PRESENCE OF PARTICLES. IF YOU
DO NOT REMOVE THE SCREEN FROM THE CHIP DETECTOR, IT IS
POSSIBLE THAT YOU WILL NOT BE ABLE TO SEE THE PARTICLES THAT
ARE CAUGHT BY THE CHIP DETECTOR MAGNET .
CAUTION
CFM DOES NOT RECOMMEND THE USE OF ADHESIVE TAPE. HOWEVER,
IF A PIECE OF ADHESIVE TAPE IS USED TO COLLECT THE PARTICLES,
MAKE SURE THE ADHESIVE IS NOT TOO STRONG. IF THE ADHESIVE ON
THE TAPE IS TOO STRONG, THE PARTICLES MAY BE DAMAGED WHEN
THEY ARE REMOVED AND/OR IT WILL AFFECT THE LABORATORY
ANALYSIS OF THE MATERIAL.
(2) Do these steps to remove the scavenge screen from the chip detector and clean the scavenge
screen (Figure 606):
79-00-00
(a) Make sure that you do not remove the particles from the chip detector magnet.
(b) Make sure that you collect all the particles in the scavenge screen while you remove the
scavenge screen from the chip detector.
(c) Place the chip detector on a clean surface, keep the plastic bag and identification tag.
(d) Push the spring-loaded pin that attaches the scavenge screen to the chip detector with a
swab, G01659.
(e) Carefully remove the scavenge screen from the chip detector.
1) Examine the chip detector and scavenge screen for particles and debris.
a) If you find particles, carefully remove and keep the particles away from the
scavenge screen.
CAUTION
DO NOT INSTALL THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE
PACKING. INSTALLATION OF THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT
THE PACKING CAN CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE
OPERATION AND A POSSIBLE ENGINE FAILURE
b) If no particles were found during the inspection of the Magnetic Chip Detector

MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR. IF YOU USE A MAGNET , IT CAN DECREASE
THE MAGNETIC PERFORMANCE OF THE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR. IF
YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE CAN OCCUR.

(6) Examine the particles from the chip detector and scavenge screen to identify the physical

SUBTASK 79-00-00-240-001-F00.
NOTE: Accumulation, of a specific volume, of particles over a series of regularly scheduled
chip detector inspections is not used as a method to identify any specific engine
failure. However, based on initial chip detector findings, reduced interval chip detector
inspections may be necessary to determine the amount, type and source of collected
particles. Particle identification is based on visual or laboratory analysis. Corrective
action is taken after you have identified the collected particles.
(a) Any quantity of material that is less than or equal to 0.004 in. (0.102 mm) in all dimension

CD-ROM 856A2683G01 to identify possible source and/or refer to Figure 608 for

5) After you get the analysis from the laboratory, then do the applicable corrective
action for the type of material that you found as defined in SUBTASK
79-00-00-240-001-F00.
NOTE: For more information on particle analysis, refer to the CFMI
Non-Destructive Test Manual, Part 9 and Part 10, or set, SPL-2450,
CD-ROM 856A2683G01.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-210-010-F00
(9) If you find non-magnetic particles that you cannot identify in the Fwd or Aft Sump scavenge
screen or in the AGB/TGB scavenge screen which are not aluminum-like, do these steps:
(a) SATISFACTORY CONDITION - If you find less than four particles with the condition
below, continue the engine in service.
1) The largest particle is less than 0.4 in. (10.2 mm) in all dimensions.
(b) UNSATISFACTORY CONDITION - Do these steps for the limits, that are given below
(Table 603):
Table 603/79-00-00-993-811-F00
PARTICLES
FOUND
LARGEST PARTICLE
DIMENSION
Any amount More than or equal to 0.4 in. (10.2 mm)
4 or more Less than 0.4 in. (10.2 mm)
1) Send the particles to the laboratory for analysis.
CAUTION
DO NOT INSTALL THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING.
INSTALLATION OF THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING
CAN CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND A
POSSIBLE ENGINE FAILURE
2) Do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector (MCD) Installation,
TASK 79-21-05-400-804-F00 or Debris Monitoring System (DMS) Detector
Installation, TASK 79-21-05-400-805-F00.
3) Operate the engine at a minimum of 70 percent N1 for 10 minutes
(TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00).
4) Do a check of the scavenge screens where the particles were found.
a) If you do not find particles, continue the engine in operation until you get the
analysis from the laboratory.
b) If you find particles which are less than 0.2 in. (5.1 mm), do these steps:
79-00-00
<1> Do a check of the scavenge screens after each flight until you get the
analysis from the laboratory.
<2> If you find non-magnetic particles which are more than 0.4 in.
(10.2 mm) during post-flight chip detector inspections, limit the engine
operation to 10 flight cycles.
c) If you find non-magnetic particles which are more than 0.2 in. (5.1 mm), limit
the engine operation to 10 flight cycles.
<1> Do a post-flight check of the chip detector and scavenge screens until
you get the analysis from the laboratory.
5) After you get the analysis from the laboratory, then do the applicable corrective
action for the type of material that you found as defined in SUBTASK
79-00-00-240-001-F00.
NOTE: For more information on particle analysis, refer to the CFM International
(CFMI) Nondestructive Test (NDT) Manual, Part 9 and Part 10, or set,
SPL-2450, CD-ROM 856A2683G01.
SUBTASK 79-00-00-420-001-F00
CAUTION
DO NOT INSTALL THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING.
INSTALLATION OF THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING CAN
CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND A POSSIBLE
ENGINE FAILURE
(10) If not already done, do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector (MCD) Installation,
TASK 79-21-05-400-804-F00 or Debris Monitoring System (DMS) Detector Installation,
TASK 79-21-05-400-805-F00.

indicator is not extended, then the malfunction is in the warning system.

F . Oil Tank Installation
SUBTASK 79-11-01-210-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare for the installation of the oil tank [5] (TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00):
(a) Make sure that the mating interfaces of the three square hose flanges and the oil-out,
oil-in and vent bosses are clean and in a good condition.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-420-001-F00
(2) Attach the top mount to the top bracket on flange B6:
(a) Lubricate the threads and the shank of the shoulder-headed pin [20] with Molykote U-n
lubricant, D00626 [CP2171].
(b) Loosen the nut [19].
(c) Put the top mount of the oil tank [5] in its position on flange B6.
(d) Install the shoulder-headed pin [20], the washer [22], and the castellated nut [21].
NOTE: Install the shoulder-headed pin with the head in the forward position.
1) Tighten the castellated nut [21] with your fingers.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-420-002-F00
(3) Attach the forward and aft bottom mounts to the brackets on flanges B4, B5 and B7:
(a) Lubricate the threads and the shank of the two shoulder-headed pins [24] and mating
faces of the two dampers [23] with Molykote U-n lubricant, D00626 [CP2171].
(b) Install the dampers [23] to the brackets on flanges B4, B5 and B7.
(c) Put the forward and aft bottom mounts on the four dampers [23].
(d) Install the two shoulder-headed pins [24] to attach the oil tank [5].
1) Torque the shoulder-headed pins [24] and the nut [19] at the same time, to 314 in-lb
(35 N·m) - 346 in-lb (39 N·m).
2) Torque the castellated nut [21] to 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m) - 120 in-lb (13.6 N·m).
79-11-01
(e) Install a new cotter pin [18] in the castellated nut [21].
NOTE: If it is necessary, loosen the castellated nut to be clear of the first slot to aid with
the installation of the cotter pin.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-420-003-F00
WARNING
DO NOT GET THIS MATERIAL IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR SKIN.
DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES FROM THIS MATERIAL. PUT ON A
RESPIRATOR, EYE PROTECTION (GOGGLES, OR OTHER APPROVED
PROTECTION), AND GLOVES BEFORE YOU USE THIS MATERIAL. MAKE
SURE THAT THERE IS SUFFICIENT AIRFLOW. KEEP THIS MATERIAL AWAY
FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND HEAT . THIS MATERIAL CAN CAUSE INJURIES
TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(4) Install the bonding strip [15] to the fan frame on flange B6:
(a) Clean the bolt [13], the washer [14], and the bonding strip [15] terminal lug and the
mating area of the fan frame with alcohol, B00676 [CP1041].
(b) Carefully clean the fasteners, terminal lug and the mating area on the fan frame with a
dry cotton wiper, G00034.
(c) Apply the jointing compound, A50447.
1) Apply a large quantity of jointing compound, A50447, to the shaft of the bolt [13]
(thread included) and below the bolt head.
2) Install the washer [14], bolt [13], spacer [16], and nut [17].
NOTE: Install the bolt head forward.
3) Torque the nut [17] to 257 in-lb (29 N·m) - 274 in-lb (31 N·m).
4) Remove the nut [17], spacer [16], bolt [13], and washer [14].
5) Remove all unwanted jointing compound, A50447, that must remain inside the
attachment hole only.
NOTE: Mating faces must be clean and dry before you apply new grease to make
sure that it is efficient.
(d) Apply a thin layer of Brisal-OX 50-855 grease, D00625 [CP2338], to the clean mating
faces.
(e) Lubricate the mating faces of the terminal lug on the bonding strip [15] and mating area
on the fan frame with a thin layer of Brisal-OX 50-855 grease, D00625 [CP2338].
(f) Install the washer [14] and bolt [13] to attach the terminal lug on the bonding strip [15] to
the fan frame flange with the spacer [16] and the nut [17].
1) Torque the nut [17] to 257 in-lb (29 N·m) - 274 in-lb (31 N·m).
(g) Remove all unwanted grease with a dry clean cloth.
NOTE: The area around the attachment holes must be clean and dry.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-420-004-F00
(5) Attach the oil-out hose [7], oil-in hose [11], and vent hose [9] to the oil tank [5]:
(a) Remove the protective caps from the hoses, tubes and oil tank openings.
(b) Lubricate the gasket [8], gasket [12], and gasket [3] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(c) Lubricate the threads of the bolt [6], bolt [10] and bolt [4] with graphite mineral vaseline,
D00601 [CP2101].
(d) Install the gasket [8] between the oil-out boss and oil-out hose [7].
79-11-01
(e) Install the four bolts [6] to attach the oil-out hose [7].
1) Torque the bolts [6] to 98 in-lb (11.1 N·m) - 110 in-lb (12.4 N·m).
(f) Install the gasket [12] between the oil-in boss and oil-in hose [11].
(g) Install the four bolts [10] to attach the oil-in hose [11].
(h) Install the gasket [3] between the vent boss and vent hose [9].
(i) Install the four bolts [4] to attach the vent hose [9].
1) Torque the bolt [10] and the bolt [4] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m).
2) Torque the oil-in coupling nut to 900 in-lb (102 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124.3 N·m).
SUBTASK 79-11-01-420-005-F00
(6) Connect the drain hose [2] to the oil tank [5]:
(a) Lubricate the threads of the oil scupper nipple [1] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(b) Connect the drain hose [2] to the bottom of the oil scupper nipple [1].
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE
ONE TO HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE
COUPLING NUT . IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE
TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
1) Tighten the drain hose [2] to 257 in-lb (29 N·m) - 284 in-lb (32.1 N·m).
2) Loosen the drain hose [2] to release the torque.
3) Tighten the drain hose [2] to 257 in-lb (29 N·m) - 284 in-lb (32 N·m).
SUBTASK 79-11-01-610-008-F00
(7) If the new oil tank did not come with a new oil quantity transmitter, do this step:
(a) Do this task: Oil Quantity Transmitter Installation, TASK 79-31-01-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-020-009-F00
(8) If the new oil tank did come with a new oil quantity transmitter, do this step:
(a) Connect the electrical connector, DP1301, to the transmitter receptacle.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-610-006-F00
(9) Do this task: Replenish the Engine Oil, TASK 12-13-11-600-801.

2. Oil Tank Inspection
(Figure 601)
A. General
(1) This task is the inspection procedure for the oil tank envelope (referred to as the envelope).
B. References
Reference Title
12-13-11-600-803 Drain the Engine Oil (P/B 301)
71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
71-11-02-410-801-F00 Close the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
79-11-01-000-801-F00 Oil T ank Removal (P/B 401)
79-11-01-300-801-F00 Replacement of the Filler Cap Packing or Filler Cap (P/B 801)
79-11-01-400-801-F00 Oil T ank Installation (P/B 401)
C. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-1238 Indicator - Dial
D. Location Zones
Zone Area
411 Engine 1 - Engine
421 Engine 2 - Engine
E. Prepare for the Oil Tank Inspection
SUBTASK 79-11-01-010-006-F00
(1) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
F . Oil Tank Inspection
SUBTASK 79-11-01-900-001-F00
(1) If you find damage that is more than the limits, replace the oil tank, unless you are given other
instructions.
These are the tasks:
• Oil T ank Removal, TASK 79-11-01-000-801-F00.
• Oil T ank Installation, TASK 79-11-01-400-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-210-002-F00
(2) Examine the envelope for damage:
(a) Cracks
1) Cracks in the envelope are not permitted.
2) Cracks in the forward bottom mount are not permitted.
3) Cracks in the aft bottom mount are not permitted.
79-11-01
(b) Nicks or scratches
1) No more than three nicks or scratches are permitted, with these conditions:
a) The depth of the nicks or scratches is no more than 0.015 inches (0.38 mm).
b) The length of the nicks or scratches is no more than 0.375 inches (9.52 mm).
c) Damage does not connect.
d) You can not feel the nicks or scratches with a 0.030 inch (0.760 mm) dial
indicator, STD-1238.
(c) Cracks or a cloudy glass window
1) Cracks or a cloudy glass window in the sight gage are not permitted.
(d) Small dents or bumps
1) No more than three small dents or bumps are permitted, with these conditions:
a) The contour is smooth.
b) The damage is not connected or aligned.
c) The capacity of the oil tank does not change.
d) The damage does not cause distortion of adjacent items that you can see.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-210-003-F00
(3) Examine the sealing interfaces between the areas that follow for leaks:
(a) The sight gage and the envelope
1) Leaks between the sight gage and the envelope are not permitted.
(b) The drain plug and the envelope boss recess
1) Leaks between the drain plug and the envelope boss recess are not serviceable.
2) If you find leaks, do these steps:
a) Torque the drain plug to 135-150 pound-inches (15.2-16.9 Newton meters).
b) If it still leaks, do this task: Drain the Engine Oil, TASK 12-13-11-600-803
and, replace the packing.
(c) The flanges of the oil-in, vent and oil-out hoses and the envelope boss
1) Leaks between the flanges are not serviceable.
2) If you find leaks, do these steps:
a) Tighten the flange bolts.
b) If it still leaks, replace the gaskets; and, if it still leaks, then replace the oil tank.
These are the tasks:
• Oil T ank Removal, TASK 79-11-01-000-801-F00.
• Oil T ank Installation, TASK 79-11-01-400-801-F00.
(d) The filler cap and the oil cap assembly
1) Leaks between the filler cap and the oil cap assembly are not serviceable.
2) If you find leaks, do these steps:
a) Make sure that the filler cap is correctly locked.
b) If the filler cap is correctly locked, replace the filler cap packing Replacement
of the Filler Cap Packing or Filler Cap, TASK 79-11-01-300-801-F00.
79-11-01
G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 79-11-01-410-002-F00
(1) Do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.

G. Oil Tank Cleaning (External)
SUBTASK 79-11-01-110-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to clean the external surface of the oil tank:
WARNING
DO NOT GET THE SOLVENT IN YOUR MOUTH, EYES, OR ON YOUR
SKIN. DO NOT BREATHE IN THE FUMES FROM THE SOLVENT . PUT ON
PROTECTIVE SPLASH GOGGLES AND GLOVES WHEN YOU USE THE
SOLVENT . KEEP THE SOLVENT AWAY FROM SPARKS, FLAME, AND
HEAT . THE SOLVENT IS POISONOUS AND FLAMMABLE, WHICH CAN
CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU SEAL ALL OIL TANK OPENINGS BEFORE YOU
START TO CLEAN. IF YOU DO NOT SEAL THE OIL TANK OPENINGS,
THEN CONTAMINATION OF THE OIL CIRCUIT CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO
THE ENGINE.
(a) Clean the outer wall of the oil tank with solvent, B00683 [CP1008], or solvent, B01028
[CP1010], or solvent, B00682 [CP2011], or alcohol, B00676 [CP1041] and a medium
nylon bristle brush, STD-3911.
(b) Dry the outer wall with an 0-30 psig dry filtered regulated air source, STD-1280 (200
kPa).
H. Oil Tank Cleaning (Internal)
SUBTASK 79-11-01-110-002-F00
(1) Do these steps to clean the internal surface of the oil tank:
(a) Fill the oil tank about 1/3 full with solvent, B00683 [CP1008], or solvent, B01028
[CP1010], or solvent, B00682 [CP2011], or alcohol, B00676 [CP1041], through the fill
port.
(b) Shake the oil tank to loosen all the contamination from the inner wall.
(c) Drain the oil tank through the drain port with a screen, STD-1271.
(d) Examine the screen for all particles of 300 microns or more.
(e) If you find particles, clean the oil tank again until there are no particles.
79-11-01
(f) Dry the inner wall with 0-30 psig dry filtered regulated air source, STD-1280 (200 kPa).
(g) Put protective covers on all the oil tank orifices General Precautions during the Removal
and Installation of Engine Components, TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00.

2. Replacement of the Filler Cap Packing or Filler Cap

A. General

(4) This procedure refers to the oil tank filler cap as the filler cap.

F . Procedure
SUBTASK 79-11-01-010-004-F00
(1) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-960-001-F00
(2) Do these steps to replace the filler cap packing [1] or the filler cap [2]:
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE OIL SCUPPER IS CLEAN. A DIRTY SCUPPER
CAN CAUSE THE CONTAMINATION OF THE OIL TANK.
(a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, to clean the oil scupper.
(b) Lift the filler cap handle.
(c) Turn the filler cap handle counterclockwise to open it.
(d) Pull the filler cap [2] from the gravity fill port.
(e) Disconnect the chain [3] from the split ring [4] on the filler cap.
(f) Remove and discard the filler cap packing [1] from the groove of the filler cap [2].
NOTE: If you find the filler cap [2] is damaged, replace it.
(g) Lubricate a new filler cap packing [1] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(h) Install the filler cap packing [1] in the groove of the filler cap [2].
1) Make sure to install the filler cap packing [1] fully in the groove.
(i) Connect the chain [3] to the split ring [4] on the filler cap [2].
(j) Put the filler cap [2] in its position in the gravity fill port.
(k) Push the filler cap [2] in and turn the handle clockwise to lock it.
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THERE IS A RISK OF INJURY TO YOUR FINGERS.
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THE HANDLE OF THE FILLER CAP CAN
ACCIDENTALLY MOVE TO ITS (UNLOCKED) VERTICAL POSITION. IF IT
DOES THIS, THERE IS A RISK THAT PRESSURIZED HOT OIL WILL
FLOW OUT OF THE TANK AND CAUSE INJURY (DANGEROUS BURNS).
(l) Push the filler cap handle down to the closed position.
G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 79-11-01-410-005-F00
(1) Do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.

SUBTASK 79-11-01-010-007-F00

79-11-01
SUBTASK 79-11-01-960-003-F00
(2) Do these steps to replace the oil cap assembly packing [24] and oil cap assembly packing [26].
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE OIL SCUPPER IS CLEAN. A DIRTY SCUPPER
CAN CAUSE THE CONTAMINATION OF THE OIL TANK.
(a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, to clean the oil scupper.
(b) Put the oil tank filler cap handle to the (unlocked) vertical position and turn the filler
cap [2] counterclockwise to open it.
(c) Remove the two bolts [22] and washers [21].
(d) Remove the oil cap assembly [23] from the oil scupper [25].
(e) Put the oil cap assembly [23] in a clean container to collect oil drippings.
(f) Remove and discard the oil cap assembly packing [24] and the oil cap assembly
packing [26] from the grooves of the oil cap assembly.
WARNING
DO NOT LET THE ENGINE OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. ENGINE OIL IS
POISONOUS. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES. USE ENGINE OIL IN AN
AREA WITH GOOD AIRFLOW. DO NOT GET ENGINE OIL ON YOUR
CLOTHES OR ON THE AIRPLANE. ENGINE OIL CAN CAUSE INJURIES.
(g) Lubricate the new oil cap assembly packing [24] and the new oil cap assembly
packing [26] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(h) Install a new oil cap assembly packing [24] in the groove of the oil cap assembly [23].
(i) Open up the flapper of the oil cap assembly [23]. Install a new oil cap assembly
packing [26] in the groove.
NOTE: Make sure that the surfaces are clean before you install the parts.
(j) Install the oil cap assembly [23] on the oil scupper [25]. Make sure that you do not
damage the new oil cap assembly packing [24].
NOTE: Make sure that the oil cap assembly [23] and the oil scupper [25] are installed in
the correct position.
(k) Install the two bolts [22] and washers [21] to attach the oil cap assembly [23] to the oil
tank.
(l) Tighten the bolts [22] to between 75 in-lb (8.5 N·m) and 85 in-lb (9.6 N·m).
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THERE IS A RISK OF INJURY TO YOUR FINGERS.
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THE HANDLE OF THE FILLER CAP CAN
ACCIDENTALLY MOVE TO ITS (UNLOCKED) VERTICAL POSITION. IF IT
DOES THIS, THERE IS A RISK THAT PRESSURIZED HOT OIL WILL
FLOW OUT OF THE TANK AND CAUSE INJURY (DANGEROUS BURNS).
(m) Push the filler cap [2] in and turn the filler cap handle clockwise to lock it. Push the filler
cap handle down to the closed position.
79-11-01

contamination (TASK 12-13-11-600-803).

WARNING

AREA WITH GOOD AIRFLOW. DO NOT GET ENGINE OIL ON YOUR

79-11-01
(f) Install a new packing [44] in the groove on the remote filling block head coupling [62] with
the tool kit 9970-957-740.
NOTE: Make sure that the surfaces are clean before you install the parts.
(g) Install the remote filling block head coupling [62] in the remote filling port (lower opening).
Tighten the coupling by hand.
NOTE: Make sure that the surfaces are clean before you install the parts.
(h) Torque the remote filling block head coupling [62] to between 181.5 in-lb (20.5 N·m) and

NOTE: Make sure that the surfaces are clean before you install the parts.
(j) Torque the over flow block head coupling [61] to between 270 in-lb (30.5 N·m) and
300 in-lb (33.9 N·m).
WARNING
DO NOT LET THE ENGINE OIL STAY ON YOUR SKIN. ENGINE OIL IS
POISONOUS. DO NOT BREATHE THE FUMES. USE ENGINE OIL IN AN
AREA WITH GOOD AIRFLOW. DO NOT GET ENGINE OIL ON YOUR
CLOTHES OR ON THE AIRPLANE. ENGINE OIL CAN CAUSE INJURIES.
(k) Lubricate the new plug [42] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(l) Lubricate the new plug [43] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(m) Attach the new plug [42] with its chain to the scupper hole and install it on the remote
filling block head coupling [62].
(n) Push the plug [42] to lock it.
(o) Attach the new plug [43] with its chain to the scupper hole and install it on the remote
filling block head coupling [62].
(p) Push the plug [43] to lock it.
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THERE IS A RISK OF INJURY TO YOUR FINGERS.
WARNING
IF THE HANDLE POSITION IS OPPOSITE TO THE OIL QUANTITY
TRANSMITTER, THE HANDLE OF THE FILLER CAP CAN
ACCIDENTALLY MOVE TO ITS (UNLOCKED) VERTICAL POSITION. IF IT
DOES THIS, THERE IS A RISK THAT PRESSURIZED HOT OIL WILL
FLOW OUT OF THE TANK AND CAUSE INJURY (DANGEROUS BURNS).
(q) Push the filler cap [2] in and turn the filler cap handle clockwise to lock it. Push the filler
cap handle down to its closed position.
SUBTASK 79-11-01-612-001-F00
(3) Do this task: Replenish the Engine Oil, TASK 12-13-11-600-801 or Replenish the Engine Oil
(Remote Fill Procedure), TASK 12-13-11-600-806.

Circuit.

that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.
Reference Description

C. Air Flow Parameters, Formula and References
SUBTASK 79-21-00-480-001-F00
(1) regulated air source, STD-3940 for the calibration of the test rig and air flow test.
(a) Clean and dry
(b) Pressure 34.5 Psia (237.5 KPa)
(c) Hose with maximum length 13 feet (4 meters) and with minimum section 0.1 square
inches (.64 sq.cm)
SUBTASK 79-21-00-970-001-F00
(2) Use this air flow formula to correct the measured air flow (in pph or kg/h only) for the calibration
of the test rig and the air flow test (Table 201).
Table 201/79-21-00-993-806-F00 Air Flow Correction Formula
Wcorrected = Wmeasured X sq.root (Tmeasured/293.15) X 34.5/Pmeasured
Parameter Definition
Wmeasured Air flow measured (pph or kg/h only)
293.15 Deg K Reference temperature for air flow, = 528 Deg Rankin (for info only, not to correct
Wmeasured, = 20 Deg C = 68 Deg F)
Tmeasured Air temperature supplied for calibration or sent into engine tube for test in Deg K or Deg R
79-21-00
Table 201/79-21-00-993-806-F00 Air Flow Correction Formula (Continued)
Wcorrected = Wmeasured X sq.root (Tmeasured/293.15) X 34.5/Pmeasured
Parameter Definition
34.5 Reference pressure in PSIA
Pmeasured Air pressure supplied for calibration or sent into engine tube for test in PSIA, If air
pressure supplied is measured in PSIG, then sum up Pambient and Pmeasured,
Pambient + Pmeasured to correct Wmeasured

(a) The air flow check varies with the restrictor criteria, so no reference is provided and is left
to the user's monitoring.

79-21-00-700-801-F00Air Flow Test of the Engine Oil CircuitPDF p. 102
Open original AMM PDF - page 102 ↗ 
3. Air Flow Test of the Engine Oil Circuit
(Figure 202, Figure 203, Figure 204, Figure 205)
A. General
(1) The air flow test is for these parts of the engine oil circuit:
(a) Forward sump
(b) Transfer Gearbox (TGB)
(c) Accessory Gearbox (AGB)
(d) Rear sump.
(2) Read the procedure to know the instructions before you try to do this procedure.
(3) The air flow test rig must be calibrated before you do this procedure.
(4) This test can be done without flushing the engine, when the engine is off the oil supply goes
back to the oil tank.
(5) Some oil can leak when the oil supply tubes are disconnected, it is necessary to clean these
areas before the engine is continued in service.
(6) Plug or cover any connections not used to prevent objects from entering the engine.
B. References
Reference Title
12-13-11-600-801 Replenish the Engine Oil (P/B 301)
71-00-00-800-811-F00 Power Plant Test Reference Table (P/B 501)
78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)

SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-002-F00
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE
IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO.
THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN
ENGINE IS STOPPED. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A
SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND
OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME
COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU.
CAUTION
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS.
IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER.
(2) Drain the oil from the lubrication unit [1] (Figure 401):
(a) Do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector (MCD) Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-806-F00 or
Debris Monitoring System (DMS) Detector Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-807-F00.
(b) Put the adapter, SPL-2446, in the lubrication unit [1] at a chip detector location.
(c) At the same time, push and turn the drain adapter 1/4 turn clockwise.
1) Let the oil drain in the container.
(d) At the same time, push and turn the drain adapter 1/4 turn counterclockwise.
(e) Pull the drain adapter down.
(f) Remove the drain adapter from the lubrication unit [1].
(g) Drain the oil from all of the other chip detector locations.
CAUTION
DO NOT INSTALL THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING.
INSTALLATION OF THE CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING CAN
CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND A POSSIBLE
ENGINE FAILURE
(h) Do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector (MCD) Installation, TASK 79-21-05-400-804-F00 or
Debris Monitoring System (DMS) Detector Installation, TASK 79-21-05-400-805-F00.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-003-F00
(3) Disconnect the oil supply tube [10] from the lubrication unit [1] (Figure 401):

(b) Disconnect the oil supply tube [10].
(c) Remove and examine the gasket [11] (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
NOTE: If the gasket is in good condition, it can be used for the subsequent installation.
1) Discard the gasket [11], if it is in unsatisfactory condition.

F . Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-01-840-001-F00
CAUTION
THE HOLLOW DRIVE SHAFT CAN STAY ATTACHED TO THE LUBE PUMP
DRIVE-SHAFT . WHEN YOU REMOVE THE LUBE-PUMP FROM THE AGB.
MAKE SURE THAT THE HOLLOW DRIVE SHAFT STAYS ON THE AGB. IF
YOU DO NOT INSTALL THE HOLLOW DRIVE SHAFT , THE ENGINE WILL NOT
HAVE SUFFICIENT LUBRICATION WHEN IT OPERATES. DAMAGE TO THE
ENGINE CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do these steps to prepare the lubrication unit [1] for the installation
(TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00):
(a) Remove all the protective caps from the connection interfaces.
(b) Make sure that all the mating interfaces to the lubrication unit [1] are clean and in good
condition.
(c) Lubricate all the gaskets with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(d) Lubricate the threads of all the bolts with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
(e) Lubricate the threads of all the nipples with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(f) Make sure that the hollow drive shaft [23] is installed in the pad drive shaft of the
Accessory Gear Box (AGB).
G. Lubrication Unit Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-01-420-001-F00
(1) Install the lubrication unit [1] (Figure 403).
(a) Lubricate the new packing [24] and new packing [25] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
79-21-01
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE PACKING CORRECTLY DURING
THE INSTALLATION PROCEDURE. IF YOU DO NOT INSTALL THE
PACKING CORRECTLY , OIL LOSS AND DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE CAN
OCCUR.
(b) Install the packing [24] in the drive shaft groove of the lubrication unit [1].
(c) Install the packing [25] in the groove of the lubrication unit [1].
(d) Put the V-clamp [21] around the lubrication unit housing.
NOTE: The V-clamp nut must be on the side of the lubrication unit by the identification
plate.
(e) Put the lubrication unit [1] in its position on the AGB.
(f) Attach the lubrication unit [1] with the V-clamp [21].
(g) Lubricate the threads of the V-clamp stud with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601
[CP2101].
1) Tighten the V-clamp nut [20] to 115 in-lb (13 N·m) - 133 in-lb (15 N·m).

OCCUR.

(b) Connect the forward sump tube [17].
1) Tighten the coupling nut to 450 in-lb (51 N·m) - 550 in-lb (62 N·m).
(c) Connect the AGB tube [14].
1) Tighten the coupling nut to 270 in-lb (31 N·m) - 300 in-lb (34 N·m).

SUBTASK 79-21-01-010-002-F00
(1) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-009-F00
(2) Remove the applicable detector from the lubrication unit, do this task: Magnetic Chip Detector
(MCD) Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-806-F00 or Debris Monitoring System (DMS) Detector
Removal, TASK 79-21-05-000-807-F00.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-017-F00
(3) Remove each sealing spool with tool set, SPL-9649, as follows (Figure 801, Figure 802):
(a) Retract the finger and lock in position with the latch.
(b) Install the tool through the lubrication unit sleeve [29] in the scavenge.
(c) Push the lubrication unit outer sealing spool [31] and release the latch.
(d) Engage the finger in the oil port to the pump.
(e) Engage the latch into the body of the tool to lock the finger in extended position.
WARNING
LOCK THE SPOOL VALVES BEFORE YOU REMOVE THE FASTENERS
FOR THE RETAINING PLATE. SPOOL VALVE EXTENSIONS CAN CAUSE
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(f) Remove the bolts [27], washers [25], nuts [24], and retaining plate [26].
79-21-01
(g) Use the wrench to remove the lubrication unit sleeve [29].
(h) Hold the body, release the latch and carefully retract the finger to release the lubrication
unit outer sealing spool assembly springs.
(i) Remove the lubrication unit sealing spool assembly.
(j) Remove and discard the packing [1] from the inner sealing spool [22].
SUBTASK 79-21-01-400-001-F00
(4) Install a new packing [1] on the inner sealing spool, do these steps:
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE PACKING IN THE CLEAN
DETECTOR LOCATION. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , THE DAMAGE TO THE
PACKING IN THE GROOVE OF THE INNER SEALING SPOOL CAN
OCCUR.
(a) Make sure that the detector location in the lubrication unit is clean.
(b) Lightly apply a layer of oil, D00599 [CP2442], on a packing [1].
(c) Put the sliding handle of the adapter, SPL-2199, in the down position.
(d) Put the packing around the top end of the tool, so that it touches the packing stops.
(e) Install the lubrication unit sleeve [29].
(f) Put the tool in the spool lubrication unit sleeve [29] with the centering pin engaged
through the hole in the inner sealing spool.
(g) Turn the tool and engage it farther in the lubrication unit sleeve [29] until the stops of the
handle touch the edge of the sleeve.
(h) Keep the handle stops against the lubrication unit sleeve [29] while you move the handle
to the up position.
(i) While you keep the handle stops against the lubrication unit sleeve [29], move the handle
to the down position.
(j) Do these steps to install the retaining plate [26]:
1) Hold the retaining plate [26] on the housing and install bolts [27], washers [25], and
nuts [24].
2) Tighten nuts [24] to 39.9 in-lb (4.5 N·m) - 48.6 in-lb (5.5 N·m).
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE SLIDING HANDLE AND THE HANDLE COME
BACK TO THEIR DOWN POSITIONS BELOW THE UNBENDING
SPRINGS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(k) Remove the installation tool from the lubrication unit.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-210-001-F00
(5) Do a check that the inner and outer sealing spools move freely in the lubrication unit housing.

SUBTASK 79-21-01-010-003-F00
(1) Do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-011-F00
(2) Remove the detectors from the lubrication unit (TASK 79-21-05-000-806-F00 or
TASK 79-21-05-000-807-F00).
SUBTASK 79-21-01-020-012-F00
CAUTION
AFTER THE REMOVAL OF THE LUBRICATION UNIT , MAKE SURE TO PUT
THE LUBRICATION UNIT ON A CLEAN TABLE. UNWANTED CONTAMINATION
CAN CAUSE AN INCORRECT INSTALLATION OF THE PACKING.
(3) Do this task: Lubrication Unit Removal, TASK 79-21-01-000-801-F00.
(a) Make sure that you do not remove the nipples installed on the ports of the lubrication unit.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-400-002-F00
(4) Do these steps to remove each check valve with the locally manufactured tool (Figure 802):
(a) Make the pusher tool and locking tools (Figure 803).
(b) Put the pusher tool into the scavenge screen hole in the lubrication unit housing
(Figure 804).
(c) Push back the outer sealing spool [31] and at the same time put the locking tool into the
oil inlet to block the valve assembly in the upper position.
(d) Remove the pusher.
(e) Do the above steps again, to install a locking tool at the other scavenge screen holes.
WARNING
LOCK THE SPOOL VALVES BEFORE YOU REMOVE THE FASTENERS
FOR THE RETAINING PLATE. SPOOL VALVE EXTENSIONS CAN CAUSE
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(f) Remove the two nuts [24], washers [25], and bolts [27] that attach the retaining plate [26]
on the lubrication unit housing.
(g) Remove the retaining plate [26].
(h) Remove the lubrication unit sleeve [29].
(i) Remove and discard the packing [28].
(j) Put the pusher into the scavenge screen hole in the lubrication unit housing.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU HOLD THE OUTER SEALING SPOOL TIGHTLY
WHEN YOU REMOVE THE LOCKING TOOL. THE SPRING LOAD IS 30.24
LBF (134.51 N). THE UNCONTROLLED RELEASE OF THE SPRING CAN
CAUSE INJURY .
(k) Push the outer sealing spool [31] and remove the locking tool from the lubrication unit
housing.
79-21-01
(l) Remove the outer sealing spool [31], inner sealing spool [22], spring [23], and spring [32].
(m) Remove and discard the packing [30] from the outer sealing spool [31] and packing [21]
from the inner sealing spool [22].
(n) Examine the inner diameter of the outer sealing spool [31] for the presence of wear:
1) If the wear is less than 0.024 in. (0.61 mm), the part is serviceable.
2) If the wear is 0.024 in. (0.61 mm) to 0.028 in. (0.71 mm), a continue-in-service
extension of 500 hours is permitted.
3) If the wear is more than 0.028 in. (0.71 mm), discard the outer sealing spool.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-400-003-F00
(5) Do these steps to install each check valve with the locally manufactured tool:
(a) Make sure that the check valve location in the lubrication unit is clean.
NOTE: If the location is not clean, it can cause an incorrect installation of the packings in
the groove of the outer and inner sealing spool.
(b) Lightly apply a layer of oil, D00599 [CP2442], on the new packing [30], new packing [21],
and new packing [28].
(c) Install the packing [30] on the outer sealing spool [31].
(d) Install the packing [21] on the inner sealing spool [22].
(e) Install the spring [32] and spring [23] in the scavenge screen hole.
(f) Install the inner sealing spool [22] on the spring [23].
1) Note the orientation of the inner sealing spool [22] (Figure 802).
(g) Install the outer sealing spool [31] on the spring [32] so that the inner sealing spool [22]
and spring [23] are fully engaged in the inner diameter of the outer sealing spool.
1) Note the orientation of the parts (Figure 802).
(h) Push the outer sealing spool [31] with the pusher and hold until you can install the locking
tool to hold the valve assembly in place.
(i) Remove the pusher.
(j) Install the packing [28] on the lubrication unit sleeve [29].
(k) Install the lubrication unit sleeve [29] in the scavenge screen hole in the lubrication unit
housing.
1) Make sure that the packing [28] is fully engaged in the housing.
(l) Do these steps to install the retaining plate [26]:
1) Hold the retaining plate [26] on the housing and install two bolts [27], washers [25],
and nuts [24].
2) Tighten the nuts [24] to 39.9 in-lb (4.5 N·m) - 48.6 in-lb (5.5 N·m).
(m) Do these steps to release the outer sealing spool [31]:
1) Push the outer sealing spool [31] with the pusher.
2) Remove the locking tool.
3) Release the outer sealing spool [31] and remove the pusher.
SUBTASK 79-21-01-210-002-F00
(6) Make sure that the inner sealing spool [22] and outer sealing spool [31] move freely in the
lubrication unit housing.

F . Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 79-21-02-840-001-F00
(1) Isolate the fuel from the fuel pump:
(a) Make sure that the electrical power is supplied (TASK 24-22-00-860-811).
(b) Make sure that the engine start lever is in the CUTOFF position.
1) Install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858, tag on the applicable engine start lever.
(c) Make sure that the ENG VALVE CLOSED and SPAR VALVE CLOSED lights on the fuel
control panel (P5 overhead panel) are dim.
NOTE: The lights for the fuel shutoff valves have three positions:
79-21-02
- Bright when the valves are in transition or when the valves do not agree with
the commanded position
- Dim when the valves are closed
- Off when the valves are opened.
(d) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.
1) Set the BAT switch on the Electrical Meters Battery and Galley Power Module
(P5-13) to the OFF position and install a DO NOT OPERATE tag, STD-858.
SUBTASK 79-21-02-010-001-F00

SUBTASK 79-21-02-010-002-F00
(3) Do this task: Servo Fuel Heater Removal, TASK 73-11-07-000-801-F00.
G. Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Removal
SUBTASK 79-21-02-020-002-F00
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE ENGINE
IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO.
THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES AFTER AN
ENGINE STOPS. A PRESSURIZED OIL SYSTEM CAN RELEASE A SPRAY OF
HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON CLOTHES, GOGGLES, AND
OTHER EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION, OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME
COOL. HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU.
CAUTION
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER COMPONENTS.
IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS ON IT . OIL CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU SEPARATE THE FUEL SERVO FROM THE HEAT
EXCHANGER DUE TO THE CLEARANCE BETWEEN THE TOP OF THE HEAT
EXCHANGER HOUSING AND THE SERVO HEATER HOUSING TO THE
ADJACENT TUBES AND THE TGB. IF YOU DO NOT , THE CONTACT WITH
THE FUEL PUMP STUDS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE HEAT-EXCHANGER
FUEL-PUMP FLANGE.
(1) Disconnect the fuel tube [7] and fuel tube [3]:
(a) Disconnect the fuel tube [3] from the fuel tube [7].
1) Let the fuel drain from the fuel tube [3] and fuel tube [7].
(b) Remove the nut [15], bolt [14], and clamp [16] that attach the fuel tube [7] to the
bracket [9].
79-21-02
RYR ALL PRE CFM56-7B-79-0023 OR PRE CFM56-7B-72-0468
(c) Remove the bolts [6] and washers [20] that attach the fuel tube [7] to the heat
exchanger [1].
RYR ALL POST CFM56-7B-79-0023 AND POST CFM56-7B-72-0468
(d) Remove the bolts [21] that attach the fuel tube [7] to the heat exchanger [1].
RYR ALL
(e) Disconnect the fuel tube [7] from the heat exchanger [1].
1) Let the fuel drain from the fuel tube [7] and heat exchanger [1].
(f) Remove and examine the gasket [8] (TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
NOTE: Use the gasket if it is in good condition.
1) Discard the gasket [8] if it is in unsatisfactory condition.
(g) Push the fuel tube [7] forward of the heat exchanger [1].
NOTE: It is necessary to move the fuel tube to get access to the fasteners and to
remove the heat exchanger. You can temporarily attach or put the fuel tube
behind a different tube.
(h) Remove the bolts [2] that attach the fuel tube [3] to the Hydro-Mechanical Unit (HMU).
(i) Remove the fuel tube [3].
(j) Remove and examine the gasket [4].
NOTE: Use the gasket if it is in good condition.
1) Discard the gasket [4] if it is in unsatisfactory condition.
(k) Install protective covers on the fuel tubes (TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00).
SUBTASK 79-21-02-020-005-F00
(2) Remove the drain tube [5]:
(a) Remove the nut [12], bolt [10], washer [11], and clamp [13] that attach the drain tube [5]
to the bracket [9].
(b) Disconnect the drain tube [5] from the heat exchanger [1].
1) Let the fluid drain from the drain tube [5] and heat exchanger [1].
(c) Disconnect the drain tube [5] from the drain manifold.
1) Let the fluid drain from the drain tube [5] and drain manifold.
(d) Remove the drain tube [5].
1) Install protective covers on the drain tube [5].
SUBTASK 79-21-02-020-006-F00
(3) Remove the heat exchanger [1]:
(a) Remove the nuts [19] and washers [18] that attach the heat exchanger [1] to the fuel
pump.
(b) Remove the heat exchanger [1] from the fuel pump.
(c) Remove and examine the gasket [17].
NOTE: Use the gasket if it is in good condition.
1) Discard the gasket [17] if it is in unsatisfactory condition.
2) Install protective covers on the fuel pump and the heat exchanger
(TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00).
79-21-02
SUBTASK 79-21-02-620-001-F00
(4) If it is necessary, do these steps for the preservation of the heat exchanger:
(a) Remove the protective plugs and caps from all the orifices.
(b) Put oil, D00599 [CP2442], in the oil and fuel circuits of the heat exchanger.
(c) Put protective plugs and caps in all the open orifices.

G. Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-02-630-001-F00
(1) If you did a preservation of the heat exchanger, drain the oil from the oil circuits and fuel
circuits.
79-21-02
SUBTASK 79-21-02-840-002-F00
(2) Do these steps to prepare the heat exchanger for the installation
(TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00):
(a) Remove all the protective covers and caps.
(b) Clean the mating interfaces of the heat exchanger flanges, adapter, fuel pump, and
adjacent areas with a cotton wiper, G00034.
(c) Make sure that the heat exchanger flanges, adapter, fuel pump, and adjacent areas are
clean and in a good condition.
(d) Lubricate the gaskets with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(e) Lubricate the threads of the bolts and studs with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601
[CP2101], before the installation.
(f) Lubricate the threads of the nipples with oil, D00599 [CP2442], before the connection.
H. Main Oil/Fuel Heat Exchanger Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-02-420-002-F00
(1) Install the heat exchanger [1] on the fuel pump:
(a) Install the gasket [17].
(b) Put the heat exchanger [1] in its position on the fuel pump.
(c) Attach the heat exchanger [1] to the fuel pump with the washers [18] and nuts [19].
1) Tighten the nuts [19] to 120 in-lb (13.6 N·m) - 140 in-lb (15.8 N·m) in a criss-cross
pattern.
SUBTASK 79-21-02-420-003-F00
(2) Install the drain tube [5]:
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO TIGHTEN THE COUPLING NUT . USE ONE TO
HOLD THE FITTING, AND THE OTHER TO TURN THE COUPLING NUT .
IF YOU DO NOT USE TWO WRENCHES, DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT
CAN OCCUR.
(a) Connect the drain tube [5] to the drain manifold.
1) Tighten the tube coupling nut to 257 in-lb (29.0 N·m) - 284 in-lb (32.1 N·m).
(b) Connect the drain tube [5] to the heat exchanger [1] as follows:
1) If not installed, install the nipple [22] with the new O-ring [23] on the heat
exchanger [1] as follows:
a) Lubricate the threads of the nipple [22] with engine oil, D00599 [CP2442].
b) Lubricate the new O-ring [23] with pure mineral vaseline, D00672 [CP5070].
c) Install the O-ring [23] into the groove of the nipple [22].
d) Install the nipple [22] in the port of the heat exchanger [1].
e) Tighten the nipple [22] to 170 in-lb (19.2 N·m) - 185 in-lb (20.9 N·m).
f) Fix the nipple [22] with a safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], safety wire, G02495
[CP8002], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006].
2) Connect the drain tube [5] to the heat exchanger [1].
3) Tighten the tube coupling nut to 257 in-lb (29.0 N·m) - 284 in-lb (32.1 N·m).
(c) Attach the drain tube [5] to the bracket [9] with the clamp [13], bolt [10], washer [11], and
nut [12].
79-21-02
1) Tighten the nut [12] or bolt [10] for the clamp [13] to 65 in-lb (7.3 N·m) - 100 in-lb
(11.3 N·m).
RYR ALL PRE CFM56-7B-79-0023 OR PRE CFM56-7B-72-0468
SUBTASK 79-21-02-420-006-F00
(3) Connect the fuel tube [7] and fuel tube [3]:
(a) Install the gasket [8].
(b) Connect the fuel tube [7] to the heat exchanger [1] with the bolts [6] and washers [20].
(c) Install the gasket [4].
(d) Put the fuel tube [3] in its position.
(e) Install the bolts [2].
(f) Connect the fuel tube [3] to the fuel tube [7].
(g) Attach the fuel tube [7] to the bracket [9] with the clamp [16], bolt [14], and nut [15].
(h) Tighten the bolts [6] as follows:
NOTE: You must apply the following steps at each of the four bolts [6] to cross-tighten
them.
Start with the first bolt at the top left side of the port.
The second bolt is at the diagonal opposite location of the first bolt.
The third bolt is at the bottom left side of the port.
The fourth bolt is at the diagonal opposite location of the third bolt.
1) Tighten the bolts [6] to 40 in-lb (4.5 N·m) - 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m).
2) Loosen the bolts [6] but do not remove them.
3) Measure the locking tighten of each bolt [6].
4) This value must be least 2 in-lb (0.2 N·m), if not, replace the bolt [6]
5) If the locking tighten is still below 2 in-lb (0.2 N·m), replace the heat exchanger.
6) Tighten the bolts [6] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m).
(i) Tighten the bolts [2] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m).
(j) Tighten the tube coupling nut to 900 in-lb (101.7 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124.3 N·m).
(k) Tighten the nut [15] to 62 in-lb (7.0 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8.0 N·m).
RYR ALL POST CFM56-7B-79-0023 AND POST CFM56-7B-72-0468
SUBTASK 79-21-02-430-001-F00
(4) Connect the fuel tube [7] and fuel tube [3]:
(a) Install the gasket [8].
(b) Connect the fuel tube [7] to the heat exchanger [1] with the bolts [21].
(c) Install the gasket [4].
(d) Put the fuel tube [3] in its position.
(e) Install the bolts [2].
(f) Connect the fuel tube [3] to the fuel tube [7].
(g) Attach the fuel tube [7] to the bracket [9] with the clamp [16], bolt [14], and nut [15].
79-21-02
RYR ALL POST CFM56-7B-79-0023 AND POST CFM56-7B-72-0468 (Continued)
(h) Tighten the bolts [21] as follows:
NOTE: You must apply the following steps at each of the four bolts [21] to cross-tighten
them.
Start with the first bolt at the top left side of the port.
The second bolt is at the diagonal opposite location of the first bolt.
The third bolt is at the bottom left side of the port.
The fourth bolt is at the diagonal opposite location of the third bolt.
1) Tighten the bolts [21] to 40 in-lb (4.5 N·m) - 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m).
2) Loosen the bolts [21] but do not remove them.
3) Measure the locking tighten of each bolt [21].
4) This value must be at least 2 in-lb (0.2 N·m), if not, replace the bolt [21].
5) If the locking tighten is still below 2 in-lb (0.2 N·m), replace the heater exchanger.
6) Tighten the bolts [21] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m).
(i) Tighten the bolts [2] to 49 in-lb (5.5 N·m) - 53 in-lb (6.0 N·m).
(j) Tighten the tube coupling nut to 900 in-lb (101.7 N·m) - 1100 in-lb (124.3 N·m).
(k) Tighten the nut [15] to 62 in-lb (7.0 N·m) - 71 in-lb (8.0 N·m).

2. Oil Supply Filter Removal
(Figure 401)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) This task is the removal procedure for the oil supply filter (referred to as the filter).
(2) The lubrication unit is located on the aft side of the accessory gearbox (AGB) at approximately
the 7:00 o'clock position.
(3) The engine has one oil supply filter installed in a cavity of the lubrication unit housing.
(4) This procedure refers to the oil supply filter housing as the filter housing.
B. References

F . Oil Supply Filter Removal
SUBTASK 79-21-03-680-002-F00
(1) Drain the filter housing (Figure 401):
(a) Put a 1 quart oil/fuel resistant container, STD-195, below the filter.
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE
ENGINE IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT OPEN THE OIL SYSTEM UNTIL THE PRESSURE GOES TO
ZERO. THE PRESSURE GOES TO ZERO APPROXIMATELY 5 MINUTES
AFTER AN ENGINE SHUTDOWN. A PRESSURIZED ENGINE CAN
RELEASE A SPRAY OF HOT OIL THAT CAN BURN YOU.
WARNING
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON YOU. PUT ON GOGGLES AND OTHER
EQUIPMENT FOR PROTECTION OR LET THE ENGINE BECOME COOL.
HOT OIL CAN BURN YOU.
CAUTION
DO NOT LET HOT OIL GET ON THE ENGINE OR OTHER
COMPONENTS. IMMEDIATELY CLEAN THE COMPONENT IF OIL FALLS
ON IT . OIL CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO PAINT AND RUBBER.
(b) Remove the drain plug [9] from the bottom of the cover [7].
1) Let the oil drain from the filter housing.
(c) Remove and discard the packing [8].
SUBTASK 79-21-03-020-002-F00
(2) Remove the filter element [4] (Figure 401):
(a) Remove the three bolts [10], washers [1] and nuts [2] that attach the cover [7] to the filter
housing.
(b) Remove the cover [7] from the filter housing with your hands.
(c) Remove and discard the packing [6] from the cover [7].
(d) Remove and discard the filter element [4], packing [3] and packing [5].
(e) Install a protective cover on the filter housing General Precautions during the Removal
and Installation of Engine Components, TASK 70-10-02-910-801-F00.

SUBTASK 79-21-03-100-001-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare the filter housing for the installation:
(a) Do this task: General Precautions during the Removal and Installation of Engine

(b) Make sure that the mating flanges to the cover [7] and the filter housing are clean and in
good condition.
79-21-03
(c) Make sure that the grooves of the new filter element [4] are clean and in good condition.
(d) Remove the protective cover from the filter housing.
G. Oil Supply Filter Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-03-420-001-F00
(1) Install the filter element [4].
(a) Lubricate the new packing [3], packing [5], packing [6], and packing [8] with oil, D00599
[CP2442].
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE PACKING CORRECTLY DURING
THE INSTALLATION PROCEDURE. IF YOU DO NOT INSTALL THE
PACKING CORRECTLY , OIL LOSS AND DAMAGE TO THE ENGINE CAN
OCCUR.
(b) Install the packing [3] and packing [5] on the filter element [4].
(c) Install the packing [6] on the cover [7].
(d) Carefully, engage the new filter element [4], with the packing [3] and packing [5], in the
filter housing.
(e) Install the cover [7] on the filter housing.
1) Make sure that the oil filter cover [7] does not interfere with the clogging indicator.
(f) Lubricate the bolts [10] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
(g) Install the bolts [10], washers [1], and nuts [2] that attach the cover [7] to the filter
housing.
1) Tighten the bolts [10] to 45 in-lb (5 N·m) - 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m).
(h) Install the packing [8] on the drain plug [9].
(i) Install the drain plug [9] on the cover [7].
1) Tighten the drain plug [9] to 45 in-lb (5 N·m) - 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m).
2) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], on the
drain plug [9].
SUBTASK 79-21-03-210-001-F00
(2) Do a visual check of the red clogging indicator [11].
(a) If you can see the red clogging indicator [11], do these steps:
1) Do the steps below to set the red clogging indicator [11].
2) Do this task: Oil Supply Filter Pop-Out Indicator Inspection (Visual Check),
TASK 79-00-00-200-805-F00.
(b) If you can not see the red clogging indicator [11], put the airplane back to its usual
condition.
SUBTASK 79-21-03-820-001-F00
(3) Set the red clogging indicator [11].
(a) Manually remove the knurled retaining ring [13].
(b) Remove the glass inspection bowl [12].
(c) Push the button on the red clogging indicator [11] to the retracted position.
NOTE: The button must stay in the retracted position.
(d) Install the glass inspection bowl [12].
79-21-03
(e) Lubricate the knurled retaining ring [13] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(f) Install the knurled retaining ring [13] with your hand.
(g) Make sure that the red clogging indicator [11] stays in its retracted position.
(h) Install safety wire, G02345 [CP8001], or safety cable, G50065 [CP8006], to the knurled
retaining ring [13] and the clogging indicator body.

G. Scavenge Oil Filter Assembly Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-04-420-001-F00

SUBTASK 79-21-04-420-002-F00
(2) Install the outlet hose [2]:
79-21-04
(a) Lubricate the four bolts [1] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
(b) Lubricate the gasket [12] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(c) Install the gasket [12] between the outlet hose [2] and the filter assembly [9].
(d) Install the four bolts [1] to attach the outlet hose [2] to the filter assembly [9].
1) Tighten the bolts [1] to 98-110 pound-inches (11.0-12.5 Newton meters).
SUBTASK 79-21-04-420-003-F00
(3) Install the inlet hose [6]:
(a) Lubricate the four bolts [5] with graphite mineral vaseline, D00601 [CP2101].
(b) Lubricate the gasket [7] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
(c) Install the gasket [7] between the inlet hose [6] and the filter assembly [9].
(d) Install the four bolts [5] to attach the inlet hose [6] to the filter assembly [9].
1) Tighten the bolts [5] to 98-110 pound-inches (11-12.5 Newton meters).
(e) Tighten the bolts [8] at the lubrication unit to 49-53 pound-inches (5.5-6 Newton meters).
SUBTASK 79-21-04-420-004-F00

(3) The engine has three MCDs in the lubrication unit.

Reference Title
79-00-00-200-804-F00 Chip Detectors and Scavenge Screens - Inspection (P/B 601)
C. Tools/Equipment

(1) Open the Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door access panels.
Number Name/Location
413BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 1
423BL Chip Detector/Pressure Relief Door, Engine 2
SUBTASK 79-21-05-100-005-F00
(2) Make sure that the area around each magnetic chip detector [6] is clean before you remove it.
G. Magnetic Chip Detector Removal
SUBTASK 79-21-05-020-005-F00
(1) Remove the magnetic chip detector [6]:
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE COMPONENTS OF THE OIL SYSTEM IF THE
ENGINE IS HOT . THESE COMPONENTS STAY HOTTER THAN OTHER
COMPONENTS. HOT COMPONENTS CAN BURN YOU.
(a) Install the puller, SPL-2447, on the magnetic chip detector [6].
CAUTION
REMOVE AND TAG ONE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR AT A TIME. THIS
CAN PREVENT AN ERROR IN IDENTIFICATION OF THE SOURCE OF
THE MATERIAL ON THE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTORS. IF YOU DO
NOT OBEY , DAMAGE CAN OCCUR.
(b) When you remove each magnetic chip detector [6], attach a tag to identify it.
1) The tags identify the plug location to each scavenge circuit (AGB/TGB Sump
Scavenge, FWD Sump Scavenge, or AFT Sump Scavenge).
(c) Push and turn the magnetic chip detector [6] 1/4 turn counterclockwise.
(d) Pull the magnetic chip detector [6] and tool assembly from the lubrication unit.
1) Make sure that you do not lose the debris from the scavenge screen [1] and
magnetic chip detector [6], to bring them for analysis if it is necessary.
2) Make sure that you do not move the particles from the magnetic chip detector [6] or
scavenge screen [1].
(e) Remove the puller, SPL-2447, from the magnetic chip detector [6].
(f) Push the spring-loaded pin [2].
(g) Remove the scavenge screen [1].
(h) Immediately, examine the magnetic plug [7] and scavenge screen [1] very carefully for
unusual or unwanted material (TASK 79-00-00-200-804-F00).
NOTE: Be careful not to remove the debris on the scavenge screen.
(i) Remove and discard the packing [3].

H. Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-05-210-004-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare the magnetic chip detector [6] for the installation:
(a) Examine the D-shaped seal [5] for tears, cuts, or other damage
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
1) Use the D-shaped seal [5] again, if it is in good condition.
(b) If there is damage to the D-shaped seal [5], replace the seal as follows:
1) Break the D-shaped seal [5].
2) Remove and discard the D-shaped seal [5] from the groove.
3) Lubricate a new D-shaped seal [5] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
4) Install the D-shaped seal [5] in the groove nearest the handle of the magnetic chip
detector [6], with the tool, SPL-2449.
NOTE: The D-shaped seal does not replace the packing. You must install the two
seals for a correct sealing of the magnetic chip detector.
79-21-05
RYR ALL; ENGINES WITH MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR (Continued)
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL A PACKING. THE INSTALLATION OF
THE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTOR WITHOUT A PACKING CAN CAUSE
OIL LOSS DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND POSSIBLE ENGINE
FAILURE.
(c) Lubricate a new packing [3] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
1) Install the packing [3] in the magnetic chip detector groove.
(d) Install the scavenge screen [1] on the magnetic chip detector [6].
I. Magnetic Chip Detector Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-05-420-005-F00
(1) Install the magnetic chip detector [6]:
(a) Install the puller, SPL-2447, on the magnetic chip detector [6].
(b) Carefully, engage the magnetic chip detector [6] in the lubrication unit.
(c) Align the bayonet pins [4] with the slots of the bayonet housing on the lubrication unit.
(d) At the same time, push and turn the magnetic chip detector [6] and tool assembly 1/4 turn
clockwise.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE MAGNETIC CHIP DETECTORS
CORRECTLY WITH THE TWO BAYONET PINS ENGAGED. MAKE SURE
THAT YOU CAN SEE THE RED STRIPES CLEARLY THROUGH THE
LUBRICATION UNIT SLOTS. THE INCORRECT INSTALLATION CAN
CAUSE OIL LOSS DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND A POSSIBLE
ENGINE FAILURE.
(e) Make sure that the magnetic chip detector [6] is correctly installed.
NOTE: The three plug handles must be aligned and in the locked position.
(f) Remove the puller, SPL-2447, from the magnetic chip detector [6].

CAUTION

(a) When you remove each DMS detector [27], attach a tag to identify it.

not necessary to disconnect the DMS detector connectors from the DMS box.
The tags identify the plug location to each scavenge circuit (AGB/TGB Scavenge,
FWD Sump Scavenge, or AFT Sump Scavenge).
(b) Disconnect the applicable electrical connector, DJB802, DJB803, or DJB804 from the
DMS box receptacle.
(c) Remove the three DMS detector [27] cables from the four spring clips.
(d) Use the remover/installer, COM-2460, to push and turn the DMS detector [27] 1/4 turn
counterclockwise.
(e) Pull the DMS detector [27] from the lubrication unit.
1) Make sure that you do not lose the debris from the scavenge screen [21] and DMS
detector [27], to bring them for analysis if it is necessary.
2) Make sure that you do not move the particles from the DMS detector [27] or
scavenge screen [21].
(f) Push the spring-loaded pin [23].
(g) Remove the scavenge screen [21].
(h) Immediately, examine the magnetic plug [22] and scavenge screen [21] very carefully for
unusual or unwanted material (TASK 79-00-00-200-804-F00).
NOTE: Be careful not to remove the debris on the scavenge screen.
(i) Remove and discard the packing [24].

G. Prepare for the Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-05-210-005-F00
(1) Do these steps to prepare the DMS detector [27] for the installation:
(a) Make sure that there is the DMS detector [27] round-head plug (small round and flat
piece on the plug handle).
1) If there is a hole, replace the DMS detector [27] as quickly as possible.
(b) Examine the D-shaped seal [25] for tears, cuts, or other damage
(TASK 70-30-01-910-802-F00).
1) Use the D-shaped seal [25] again, if it is in good condition.
(c) If there is damage to the D-shaped seal [25], replace the seal as follows:
1) Break the D-shaped seal [25].
2) Remove and discard the D-shaped seal [25] from the groove.
3) Lubricate a new D-shaped seal [25] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
4) Install the D-shaped seal [25] in the groove nearest the cables of the DMS
detector [27] with the locally made seal fitting tool, STD-26096 (Figure 403).
NOTE: The D-shaped seal does not replace the packing. You must install the two
seals for a correct sealing of the DMS detector.
CAUTION
DO NOT INSTALL THE DMS DETECTOR WITHOUT THE PACKING.
INSTALLATION OF THE DMS DETECTOR WITHOUT A PACKING CAN
CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND POSSIBLE
ENGINE DAMAGE.
(d) Lubricate a new packing [24] with oil, D00599 [CP2442].
79-21-05
RYR ALL; ENGINES WITH DEBRIS MONITORING SYSTEM (Continued)
1) Install the packing [24] in the DMS detector groove.
(e) Install the scavenge screen [21] on the DMS detector [27].
H. DMS Detector Installation
SUBTASK 79-21-05-420-006-F00
(1) Install the DMS detector [27]:
(a) Use remover/installer, COM-2460, to carefully engage the DMS detector [27] in the
lubrication unit.
(b) Align the bayonet pins [26] with the slots of the bayonet housing on the lubrication unit.
(c) At the same time, push and turn the DMS detector [27] 1/4 turn clockwise.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU INSTALL THE DMS DETECTORS CORRECTLY
WITH THE TWO BAYONET PINS ENGAGED. MAKE SURE THAT YOU
CAN SEE THE RED STRIPES CLEARLY THROUGH THE LUBRICATION
UNIT SLOTS. INCORRECT INSTALLATION CAN CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE
DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND A POSSIBLE ENGINE SHUTDOWN.
(d) Make sure that the DMS detector [27] is correctly installed.
NOTE: The three plug handles must be aligned and in the locked position.
SUBTASK 79-21-05-420-007-F00
(2) Install the three DMS detector [27] cables:
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE ROUTING OF THE DMS DETECTOR HARNESS
IS CORRECT . INCORRECT HARNESS ROUTING CAN CAUSE THE
DETECTOR TO DISENGAGE FROM THE LUBE RECESS UNIT . THIS CAN
CAUSE OIL LEAKAGE DURING ENGINE OPERATION AND POSSIBLE
ENGINE DAMAGE.
(a) Install the three DMS detector [27] cables in the four spring clips (Figure 402).
NOTE: Start with the spring clip that is nearest to the DMS detector and end with the
spring clip that is nearest to the DMS box connector. Do not cross the DMS
detector cables.
(b) Connect the electrical connector DJB802, DJB803, or DJB804 to the applicable DMS box
receptacle.

2. Scavenge Oil Filter Element Removal
(Figure 401)
NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.
A. General
(1) This task is the removal procedure for the scavenge oil filter element (referred to as the filter
element).
(2) The scavenge oil filter assembly is attached to the aft face of the accessory gearbox (AGB) at
the 7:00 o'clock position.
(3) Each scavenge oil filter assembly has a scavenge oil filter element and a filter bowl.
(4) The scavenge oil filter element is in the filter bowl of the scavenge oil filter assembly.
(5) This procedure refers to the scavenge oil filter assembly as the filter assembly.
